Wizz

97 findings · 38 endpoints · 33.36s scan · generated 2026-09-30T20:46:36Z

Frameworks: MASVS 2.1.0, MASTG 2.0.0, MASWE 1.0.0, ATT&CK Mobile 18.1, CVSS 4.0

Scanner notes

  • Main binary is FairPlay-encrypted, TEXT-segment strings are unavailable. Resource files, symbols, and dylibs were still scanned for secrets/endpoints. Drop a decrypted .ipa (frida-ios-dump / bagbak output) for full TEXT coverage.
  • Code strings are readable in 8 of 85 binaries; 57 rules that search only code strings saw those binaries alone.
  • No embedded.mobileprovision found, typical for App Store builds re-signed by the store.
  • Noise suppressions applied (1): ignore-stdlib-debug-paths

Clusters are OWASP MASVS 2.1.0 control groups, plus Apple App Store policy obligations MASVS does not cover. Indented rows are OWASP MASWE 1.0.0 weakness classes; a finding reaches every weakness it is bound to, so they may sum above the cluster total. A weakness row counts only findings that evidence the weakness: bare API references count toward their cluster, and mitigations are listed separately below.

Findings (97)

MEDIUMSECH-01.allows_arbitrary_loads · App Transport Security globally disabled (NSAllowsArbitraryLoads)

ATS is turned off app-wide, permitting plaintext HTTP and weak TLS to any host. Traffic can be intercepted/modified on hostile networks.

Vector
SECH-01
Why this severity
NSAllowsArbitraryLoads removes the HTTPS floor for every domain, so any request the app makes over http:// or weak TLS can be read and altered on the path. Connections that use HTTPS stay encrypted, and the scan cannot say what, if anything, the app sends in cleartext, so confidentiality and integrity are scored low (convention point 2); the breadth - every domain rather than listed exceptions - does not change what is exposed per request.
CVSS
CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:H/AT:N/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:L/VA:N/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N
MASVS
MASVS-NETWORK
MASVS controls
MASVS-NETWORK-1
MASWE
MASWE-0026
MAS profiles
MAS-L1 · Essential Security MAS-L2 · Advanced Security MAS-EUDIW · EU Digital Identity Wallet
MASTG
MASTG-TEST-0322 MASTG-TEST-0342
CWE
CWE-319
Remediation (developer): Remove NSAllowsArbitraryLoads. Serve all traffic over HTTPS with TLS 1.2+, and scope any unavoidable exception to a specific domain via NSExceptionDomains.
For users: On untrusted Wi-Fi, apps with weak transport security can leak data. Avoid sensitive actions on public networks when using such apps.
Evidence (1)
KindValueSource
plistKeyNSAllowsArbitraryLoads=YESInfo.plist

INFODCS-01.user_defaults_appgroup_fingerprint · UserDefaults shared within an App Group (declared reason 1C8F.1) reference

A privacy manifest declares required reason 1C8F.1 for the UserDefaults API: the code reads and writes defaults shared with the apps, extensions and App Clips in the same App Group. Apple permits this only within the group and forbids using it for tracking. Inventory: it shows a shared store exists, not what is stored in it. (The rule id is kept for continuity; it used to fire on any UserDefaults declaration and claimed an advertising-ID share nothing in the bundle showed.)

INFODCS-04.face_id_biometrics · FaceID / LocalAuthentication biometrics reference

LocalAuthentication / LAPolicy biometric checks. The app receives only success or failure, never biometric data. Whether the check is event-bound (MASWE-0020) needs the evaluatePolicy call site, which sits in encrypted code, so this records the API for review and is not a weakness on its own.

Vector
DCS-04
Why this severity
LocalAuthentication returns only success or failure; no biometric data reaches the app (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/localauthentication). Whether the check is event-bound and bypassable (MASWE-0020) depends on the evaluatePolicy call site MASTG-TEST-0266 reads, which FairPlay encrypts, so the rule records the API and nothing more.

INFOPLAT-01.user_script_injection · WKUserScript injection reference

WKUserScript injects JS at document start/end on every load, useful for telemetry and credential interception.

Vector
PLAT-01
Why this severity
The app injects a user script into its WebView.
MASVS
MASVS-PLATFORM
MASVS controls
MASVS-PLATFORM-2 MASVS-STORAGE-2
MASWE
MASWE-0033
MAS profiles
MAS-L1 · Essential Security MAS-L2 · Advanced Security MAS-EUDIW · EU Digital Identity Wallet
CWE
CWE-749 CWE-94

INFOPLAT-01.webview_inspectable · Web Inspector toggle set on a WebView or JavaScript context (isInspectable) reference

Calls the isInspectable setter of WKWebView or JSContext (iOS 16.4+). When the value is true in a release build, Safari Web Inspector can attach over USB. The scan sees the call; the value is in FairPlay-encrypted code.

Vector
PLAT-01
Why this severity
The isInspectable setter of a WebView or JavaScript context is called. Safari Web Inspector attaches only when the value is true in the release build; the value is in FairPlay-encrypted code, so the scan records the call (MASWE-0063).
MASVS
MASVS-PLATFORM
MASVS controls
MASVS-PLATFORM-2 MASVS-RESILIENCE-4
MASWE
MASWE-0063
MAS profiles
MAS-R · Resilient Security MAS-EUDIW · EU Digital Identity Wallet
CWE
CWE-489
Frameworks
OguryAds
Affected binaries
Frameworks/OguryAds.framework/OguryAds
For users: Avoid typing passwords on in-app web screens. Open sensitive links in Safari instead, where you can verify the address bar.

INFOPLAT-02.custom_url_schemes · Registers 4 custom URL schemes (inbound deep-link surface) reference

The app registers custom URL scheme(s) via CFBundleURLSchemes. Any app or web page can invoke these; if application(_:open:options:) acts on unvalidated parameters, this is an entry point for unauthorized actions. Custom schemes are also unverified (unlike Universal Links). Review the handler for input validation.

Vector
PLAT-02
Why this severity
The app registers custom URL schemes. MASWE-0029 makes them a weakness when a sensitive flow relies on them or the handler does not validate input, which MASTG-TEST-0370 and -0371 read from the handler code.
MASVS
MASVS-PLATFORM
MASVS controls
MASVS-CODE-4 MASVS-PLATFORM-1 MASVS-STORAGE-2
MASWE
MASWE-0029
MAS profiles
MAS-L1 · Essential Security MAS-L2 · Advanced Security MAS-EUDIW · EU Digital Identity Wallet
CWE
CWE-939
Remediation (developer): Validate and sanitize every parameter in application(_:open:options:); never trigger sensitive actions from a URL without authentication. Prefer Universal Links (verified) over custom schemes.
For users: Be wary of links that open this app from messages or websites; don't tap deep links from untrusted sources.

INFOPLAT-04.background_modes · Background modes declared: fetch, location, processing, remote-notification

Legal iOS background-execution channels. `location`/`voip` enable persistent background execution and are the strongest signal; `audio`/`external-accessory` are moderate; `fetch`/`remote-notification`/`processing` are routine and declared by most apps.

Vector
PLAT-04
Why this severity
Background execution and push modes. None of the WP248 criteria is met by the mode alone.
MASVS
MASVS-PLATFORM
LINDDUN
Detecting
Solove
Surveillance
GDPR
Art. 6(1)
Remediation (developer): Declare only the background modes the app actually needs; each is reviewed by App Review and must map to genuine user-facing functionality, not background telemetry.
For users: Limit how the app wakes itself: turn off Background App Refresh and revoke unused Location/Notification permissions in Settings.

INFOPLAT-04.silent_remote_notification · Content-available silent push

content-available=1 silent push lets the server wake the app to refresh data without a visible notification. The `didReceiveRemoteNotification` delegate method appears in almost every push-using app, so it's no longer a primary signal; the silent variant is the `content-available` payload and the `remote-notification` UIBackgroundModes entry together.

INFOSECH-01.cfstream_plaintext_socket · Raw CFStream / NSStream socket (bypasses ATS) reference

Opens a raw socket stream via CFStreamCreatePairWithSocketToHost / NSStream getStreamsToHostWithName. These APIs are not governed by App Transport Security, so TLS is opt-in, a common way cleartext traffic slips past ATS. Confirm the SSL security level is set.

Vector
SECH-01
Why this severity
CFStream and NSStream sockets are not covered by ATS. MASTG-TEST-0323 fails only when such a socket carries cleartext, which depends on whether the code enables TLS on it; that code is FairPlay-encrypted, so the scan records the API.

INFOSECH-01.hardcoded_http_urls · Hard-coded cleartext http:// URLs (4 hosts)

The bundle contains http:// URLs for 4 host(s): crl.pki.goog, ocsp.pki.goog, pki.amazon.com, tizen.org. A hard-coded cleartext URL is a place the app may talk to a server without TLS. Whether it does depends on how the URL is used and on the ATS configuration, which blocks cleartext loads through URLSession unless an exception allows them.

Vector
SECH-01
Why this severity
MASTG notes that an http:// URL in the bundle does not show it is used for communication; the scan records the candidates.

INFOSECH-03.insecure_randomness · Non-cryptographic random number generator reference

Imports rand() / srand() / random() / drand48(), predictable PRNGs unsuitable for tokens, keys, IVs or nonces. Use SecRandomCopyBytes or arc4random_buf. `arc4random() % n` also introduces modulo bias; prefer arc4random_uniform(n).

Vector
SECH-03
Why this severity
A non-cryptographic generator is linked. MASTG-TEST-0311 fails only when its output is used for keys, tokens or other security values, which is in FairPlay-encrypted code, so the scan records the API.

INFOSECH-03.weak_hash · Weak hash primitive (MD5 / SHA-1) reference

Imports CC_MD5 / CC_SHA1 (CommonCrypto) or CryptoKit's Insecure.MD5 / Insecure.SHA1. Both are collision-broken and unsuitable for signatures, integrity checks or password hashing, but are also widely used for non-security purposes (cache keys, ETags, file checksums), so the finding is a pointer for review.

Vector
SECH-03
Why this severity
MD5 or SHA-1 is linked. MASTG-TEST-0211 fails only when the hash protects sensitive data; checksums and cache keys pass. That use is in FairPlay-encrypted code, so the scan records the API.

INFOSECH-04.debug_symbols · Debug symbols left in binaries (62)

Binaries carrying 50099 debugger (stab) entries in their symbol table: Alamofire, AppAuth, AppCheckCore, AppLovinQualityService, ComScore, ContextSDK. Stabs name source files, object files and functions, and often the build machine's paths. They make reverse engineering easier and are not needed to run the app.

INFOSECH-06.dynamic_loader_imports · Dynamic loader in use (dlopen / dlsym) reference

The binary imports dlopen/dlsym, so it can resolve and call code chosen at runtime rather than at link time. That is how optional frameworks, plugin architectures and A/B-tested SDKs are loaded, and it is entirely normal, so this ships as INFO inventory rather than a weakness. It matters only as context: an app that also carries obfuscation (SURV-04) or a data broker (TRK-02) can assemble behaviour a static scan cannot see, and dynamic code loading is the mechanism that makes that possible. Note the limit of the signal: an import proves the symbol is linked, not that it is ever called, and iOS forbids loading code that is not already in the signed bundle. (MASWE-0049)

Vector
SECH-06
Why this severity
dlopen/dlsym are present in the binary.
MASVS
MASVS-CODE
MASVS controls

INFOSECH-08.sdk_owner.adjust · Embedded SDK provenance: Adjust reference

Third-party code from Adjust (DE) is embedded via 1 SDK(s): Adjust. Each embedded SDK is a supply-chain dependency that can collect data and update its own behaviour.

Vector
SECH-08
Why this severity
An inventory of who owns the embedded SDKs. Ownership is not a weakness; data-broker and AI recipients carry their own findings under TRK-02 and TRK-05.
MASVS
MASVS-CODE
Remediation (developer): Inventory embedded SDKs and their data collection; remove unused SDKs and pin/verify their distribution to reduce supply-chain exposure.
For users: Bundled third-party SDKs widen who gets your data and the app's supply-chain risk. Prefer apps with fewer embedded trackers; keep the app updated.
Evidence (2)
KindValueSource
urlhttps://consent.adjust.com(main)
urlhttps://consent.eu.adjust.com(main)
References:
  • https://developer.apple.com/support/third-party-SDK-requirements/

INFOSURV-01.getifaddrs_import · Imports getifaddrs() for network interface enumeration

Imports the C function getifaddrs() which lists every network interface. NOTE: getifaddrs is also the standard primitive for plain reachability and local-IP discovery and is linked by many networking SDKs, so the import alone is a weak VPN signal that fires on most apps. MASTG / privacy-guide VPN detection keys on NEVPNManager or an explicit tunnel-interface name (utun/ppp/ipsec), kept here as an informational marker only.

Vector
SURV-01
Why this severity
Reading network interfaces and proxy settings on the device meets none of the WP248 criteria by itself.

Art. 6(1)

For users: If you use a VPN or privacy tool, be aware this app can detect it and may change behaviour or block you. No user setting disables this detection.

INFOSURV-02.dyld_image_introspection · dyld image-list introspection reference

Calls _dyld_image_count / _dyld_get_image_name, can be used to detect injected dylibs (tweaks, Frida gadget). NOTE: these symbols are also used by crash reporters, analytics SDKs and the Swift runtime to enumerate loaded images, so bare presence is not anti-analysis. OWASP MASTG anti-debugging tests key on ptrace / sysctl(P_TRACED) rather than dyld enumeration, so this is an informational capability marker, not a weakness.

INFOSURV-02.jailbreak_file_paths · Jailbreak filesystem paths probed mitigation

Hard-codes paths that exist only on jailbroken devices. The narrow primary pattern targets paths that are *only* jailbreak-relevant (Cydia, apt, the MobileSubstrate dylibs and jailbreakd). Broader literals such as `/bin/bash` or `/etc/apt` regularly appear in stock debug strings and are handled by the `ignore-stdlib-debug-paths` suppression; they only re-surface when these higher-signal paths also appear in the same binary.

INFOTGAP-01.manifest_data_type_invalid · Privacy manifest declares collected-data values Apple does not define (3)

NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes uses types or purposes that are not on Apple's lists: Collects battery level, charging state, and low memory warnings to monitor device health during Amazon Chime SDK meeting sessions. This helps diagnose session quality issues and improve overall performance and reliability., an empty string (""). Xcode combines the bundle's privacy manifests into the report developers use to fill in the App Store privacy label, and these values are not ones that report defines.

INFOTGAP-01.undeclared_tracking_domains · 1 tracker domain reached but not declared in the privacy manifest

The app ships a PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy declaring 13 tracking domain(s), but 1 further tracker-classified host appear in the bundle and are not covered by any declaration (PubMatic). iOS makes requests to the domains listed in NSPrivacyTrackingDomains fail when the user has not granted App Tracking Transparency permission; a tracking domain missing from the list is not blocked, so the user's choice does not reach it. A domain is treated as declared when it matches exactly or is a subdomain of a declared entry. MASTG-TEST-0281 fails on exactly this.

Vector
TGAP-01
Why this severity
The manifest declares tracking domains but misses some the app reaches, which fails MASTG-TEST-0281. iOS blocks only the declared domains when ATT permission is withheld, so the missing ones keep working. A declaration gap against Apple's rules and GDPR Art. 13; no standard grades it, so it is info.
MASVS
MASVS-PRIVACY
MASVS controls
MASVS-PRIVACY-3
MASWE
MASWE-0074
MAS profiles
MAS-P · Baseline Privacy MAS-EUDIW · EU Digital Identity Wallet
MASTG
MASTG-TEST-0281
CWE
CWE-359
LINDDUN
Unawareness & Unintervenability Non-compliance
Solove
Exclusion
GDPR
Art. 5(1)(a) Art. 13
Remediation (developer): Add every host that carries tracking traffic to NSPrivacyTrackingDomains, or stop contacting it on a tracking code path. Apple's guidance is that the list must cover the domains the app reaches for tracking purposes, not just the SDKs it recognises.
For users: A disclosure/compliance gap rather than a user control. Treat undisclosed data use as a caution sign when deciding whether to trust the app.

INFOTGAP-03.declared_age_range_entitlement · Declared Age Range entitlement (positive disclosure signal) mitigation

The app declares com.apple.developer.declared-age-range, Apple's API for asking the system for a user's age bracket without collecting a birth date. Using it is the privacy-preserving way to age-gate, so this is a positive signal reported as inventory.

INFOTRK-04.egress.de · Endpoints owned by DE-based organisations (6 endpoints)

The bundle references 6 endpoint(s) whose owner is based in DE, owners: Adjust, Verve. The country is the owning organisation's home jurisdiction from the tracker database, not where a server is hosted, and a referenced endpoint is not observed traffic. If the app sends personal data there, it is a third-country transfer: whether that is lawful depends on the safeguards, purpose and paperwork behind it, none of which is visible in a binary. DE holds an EU adequacy decision, so a transfer there needs no additional Chapter V safeguard. Tracker-class endpoints are among the destinations.

Vector
TRK-04
Why this severity
Where the endpoint owner is registered. International transfer is GDPR Chapter V, not a WP248 criterion.
MASVS
MASVS-PRIVACY
LINDDUN
Data Disclosure
Solove
Secondary Use
GDPR
Art. 44 Art. 46 Art. 49
For users: Your data may be sent to servers in other countries with different privacy laws. Review the privacy policy before sharing sensitive information.

INFOTRK-04.egress.us · Endpoints owned by US-based organisations (9 endpoints)

The bundle references 9 endpoint(s) whose owner is based in US, owners: Amazon, Apple, Comscore, Meta. The country is the owning organisation's home jurisdiction from the tracker database, not where a server is hosted, and a referenced endpoint is not observed traffic. If the app sends personal data there, it is a third-country transfer: whether that is lawful depends on the safeguards, purpose and paperwork behind it, none of which is visible in a binary. US holds no EU adequacy decision, so a transfer there needs an Art. 46 safeguard or an Art. 49 derogation. Tracker-class endpoints are among the destinations.

INFOmeta.fairplay_main · Main executable is FairPlay-encrypted reference

The main Mach-O is encrypted by the App Store. Static string scanning of its TEXT segment won't work without a decrypted dump (e.g., via frida-ios-dump or bagbak on a jailbroken device). Embedded frameworks and dylibs are USUALLY ENCRYPTED TOO (measured 91% across the reference corpus; in half of apps every Mach-O is encrypted), so TEXT-segment string recall is limited bundle-wide, not just for the main binary. What still survives encryption is __LINKEDIT: symbols, imported/defined symbol names, ObjC selectors and linked dylibs, which is why symbol-based detection carries this scan rather than string matching.

Vector
SURV-02
Why this severity
Scan metadata: what the scanner could and could not see.
MASVS
MASVS-RESILIENCE
Solove
Surveillance Intrusion
Remediation (developer): For full TEXT-segment coverage, supply a decrypted .ipa produced by frida-ios-dump or bagbak from a jailbroken device. Resource files, plists, entitlements, symbols, selectors and linked dylibs are still scanned in the meantime.
For users: Usually defensive (anti-fraud / anti-tamper). If the app refuses to run on your device for no clear reason, this is often why. No user action needed.
Evidence (1)
KindValueSource
fileWizzWizz
References:
  • https://mas.owasp.org/MASTG/techniques/ios/MASTG-TECH-0054/#decrypting-the-app-binary
